### cricut-material-holder spec
# Cricut Mat Holder — recovered design intent

Recovered from `production/inputs/20260630/Cricut Mat Holder.step` into an
editable, **fully-parametric** CadQuery project (no frozen residual).

## What it is

A **2-piece flat-pack stand** — an L-shaped assembly of two disjoint bodies that
slot together (no boolean between them):

| solid | name   | role                     | footprint (mm)        | extrude            | volume (mm³) |
|-------|--------|--------------------------|-----------------------|--------------------|--------------|
| 0     | `arm`  | flat blade base          | 236.2 × 30.7 (X-Y)    | +Z, 12 mm thick    | 30 861.5     |
| 1     | `post` | slim slotted column      | 29.6 × 6.0 (X-Y)      | +Z, 12→204 mm      | 23 265.5     |

`introspect.py` reports **both solids 100 % analytic, 0 % freeform** (only planes
and cylinders). So this is a clean parametric rebuild — every surface is live
code, **nothing is wrapped/frozen**.

- **arm** lies flat (z 0–12). Its blade silhouette is a long, gently-curved
  taper (one r780 mm arc forms the bottom edge), with a **mounting slot** near
  the thick tail end and a **hook notch** cut near the root.
- **post** stands at the tail end (z 12–204). It is a near-symmetric trapezoidal
  column (wide back face, rounded slant sides) with a long **through-window**, a
  **foot tab** at the bottom and a matching **cap tab** at the top. The foot tab
  is thinner than the column and lines up with the arm's mounting slot, so the
  post drops through the arm — a knock-down (flat-pack) joint.

The two bodies are returned as a `cq.Assembly` (named `arm` / `post`), placed at
their measured world positions; the master STEP flattens that to a 2-solid
compound, matching the source.

## Decomposition (per solid)

**arm (solid 0)** — `build_arm()`: extrude the recovered X-Y blade outline
(`profiles.ARM_OUTER`, 22 line/arc segments) by `arm_thickness`, then punch two
through cutters: `profiles.ARM_SLOT` (the parallelogram mounting slot) and
`profiles.ARM_HOOK` (the hook notch). The blade outline is an organic designed
curve, so it is recovered as exact ordered line/arc data rather than a few
intent dimensions (see "Wrap honesty").

**post (solid 1)** — `build_post()`:
- column: extrude the recovered X-Y outer outline (`profiles.POST_MAIN`,
  trapezoid with short back-corner flats + four r1.5 slant rounds) by
  `main_height`, placed in its Z band, then cut the through-window (a box
  centred at `slot_center_x/z`, sized `slot_width × slot_height`, cut across Y).
- foot tab + cap tab: a thinner trapezoid (`profiles.POST_TAB_*`, built
  parametrically from `tab_back_y` / `tab_depth`) extruded `tab_height`, one
  below the column (`post_base_z`) and one above (mirrored on top), `fast_union`ed
  to the column.

All booleans use `fast_cut` / `fast_union` from `booleans.py` (fuzzy + parallel)
per the recovery skill.

## Editable parameters (`params.py`)

All 10 are LIVE (verified by the editability gate):

- `arm_thickness` (12) — blade thickness (Z extrude).
- `post_base_z` (12) — bottom of the foot tab; shifts the whole column in Z.
- `tab_height` (12) — height of the foot tab and cap tab.
- `main_height` (168) — height of the slim column between the tabs.
- `slot_width` (7.6), `slot_height` (155.2) — the through-window size.
- `slot_center_x` (188.5), `slot_center_z` (108) — the window position.
- `tab_back_y` (16.1), `tab_depth` (3.7) — the foot/cap tab thickness in Y.

## Gate results

- **Fidelity (per solid, boolean-free: volume + COM + inertia)** — both PASS:
  - solid 0 (arm): principal-moment error **0.0023 %**, COM shift < 0.001 mm.
  - solid 1 (post): principal-moment error **0.0075 %**, COM shift < 0.001 mm.
  - Residual (original − scaffold) and overshoot (scaffold − original) are both
    **< 0.01 %** per solid (arm exactly 0), confirming the scaffold *is* the part.
- **Editability** — PASS: **10 LIVE, 0 DEAD, 0 ERROR**.
- **Reconstructability** — **N/A**: the recovery is fully parametric with no
  frozen residual, so nothing was punted into a wrap.
- **Topology (per solid)** — both PASS: surface-area delta **0.0008 %** (arm) /
  **0.003 %** (post), **0 redundant seams**. One *advisory* (non-gated) census
  delta on the arm: a single hook-notch corner round re-parametrized r0.2 ↔ r0.3
  (17.6 mm², ~0.05 % of area) — a benign threePointArc re-fit of a ~r0.25 round;
  the mass gate certifies equivalence.

## Wrap honesty / known omissions

There is **no frozen residual** — every shipped surface is live parametric code.

Honest scope note: the **arm blade outline** and the **post column outline** are
recovered as exact line/arc *segment data* (`profiles.py`), not as a handful of
named intent dimensions. They are organic/rounded silhouettes (the blade's r780
taper, the column's four r1.5 slant rounds) where a depth/width-parametric form
would not reproduce the measured curve to tolerance. They remain **fully analytic
and hand-editable** (edit a coordinate, re-run) — they are simply not exposed as
single knobs. The dimensions a downstream editor actually reaches for (thickness,
heights, the window, the tabs) *are* parameters.

## STEP pcurves

`export_step.py` requests pcurve-free output (`write.surfacecurve.mode = 0`), but
this OCCT build ignores the flag (`grep -c PCURVE` stays non-zero — a known no-op
in this kernel). The solid is exact and the file is small (0.2 MB); pcurves are a
reader-side cache, reprojected identically on import.

## Assumptions a downstream editor should confirm

- The two bodies are treated as a **free arrangement** (built at measured world
  positions), not a solved joint — the foot tab's fit through the arm slot is a
  measured ~0.2 mm-clearance match, not an enforced relationship.
- `tab_height` drives both the foot tab and the cap tab (measured equal).
- The through-window is treated as **centred** in the column footprint
  (`slot_center_*` defaults are the measured centre).

## Regenerating

```bash
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_step.py .   # -> cricut-material-holder.step
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_stl.py  .   # -> cricut-material-holder.stl
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python check_liveness.py . # re-check editability
```
Edit dimensions in `params.py` (or the silhouette data in `profiles.py`); never
hand-edit the STEP/STL.

### cricut-material-holder params
"""params.py — editable dimensions for the recovered Cricut Mat Holder.

Two flat-pack parts (an assembly, not a boolean):
  • arm  — a flat blade that lies down; its tail end has a slot the post foot
           drops through, and a hook-notch near the root.
  • post — a slim trapezoidal column with a long through-window, a foot tab at
           the bottom and a matching cap tab at the top.

Every field here drives geometry in main.py. The blade SILHOUETTE itself is
recovered fixed data (see profiles.py); the dimensions a user edits live here.
Counts would be typed `int`; all knobs here are continuous `float`.
"""
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Params:
    # ---- ARM (solid 0) --------------------------------------------------- #
    arm_thickness: float = 12.0        # blade thickness, extruded along +Z

    # ---- POST (solid 1) -------------------------------------------------- #
    # The column's outer outline is recovered fixed data (profiles.POST_MAIN);
    # its editable knobs are the heights, the through-window, and the tabs.
    post_base_z: float = 12.0          # bottom of the foot tab (rests on the arm top)
    tab_height: float = 12.0           # height of the foot tab and of the cap tab
    main_height: float = 168.0         # height of the slim column between the tabs

    # through-window in the column (centred in the column footprint)
    slot_width: float = 7.6            # window size across X
    slot_height: float = 155.2         # window size along Z
    slot_center_x: float = 188.5       # window centre, X
    slot_center_z: float = 108.0       # window centre, Z

    # foot / cap tab (thinner in Y than the column, drops through the arm slot)
    tab_back_y: float = 16.1           # y of the tab's back face
    tab_depth: float = 3.7             # front-to-back thickness of the tab

### crossbow-kit-card-fully-printed spec
# CAD Spec — Crossbow Kit Card (fully printed)

## Object

A single flat 86 x 54 x 2mm 3D-printable "kit card": a sacrificial outer
frame holding every part of a pocket-size crossbow, already laid out at its
final 2D profile and connected to the frame (or a neighbouring part) by
small snap-off support tabs. The user prints the whole card as one piece,
snips the tabs with a wire cutter, and assembles the parts into a working
mini crossbow (see `design_readme.md`). Source: 37 solids in one STEP file,
deduplicated to 28 canonical shapes (frame, bow limb, nock bar, 2 mirrored
stock plates, 3x pin-socket clip, trigger, ladder/crossbar, 2x guide rail,
19 support-tab canonicals).

## Coordinate system

- XY is the card's face; Z is the print/extrude thickness (0..2mm, most
  parts are thinner than the full 2mm card).
- Origin is the card's own center (matches the source STEP's origin).

## Parts (`main.py` builder functions)

- `build_frame` — sacrificial outer border. Fully parametric, zero residual.
- `build_bow_limb` — the springy top arc. Parametric scaffold + residual
  (see "Residual-frozen regions").
- `build_nock_bar` — straight bar under the bow, 2 end slots. Fully
  parametric, negligible residual.
- `build_stock_plates` — the "L" and "R" body side plates (mirrored, shared
  builder). Parametric scaffold + residual per plate.
- `build_trigger` — the sear/trigger. Fully parametric (hook profile +
  pivot hole), negligible residual.
- `build_pin_sockets` — 3 identical pin-retention clips. Parametric
  scaffold + small shared residual (a neck fillet, see below), instanced
  3x via `socket_pitch_y`.
- `build_ladder` — crossbar connecting the bow assembly to the stock.
  Fully parametric, negligible residual.
- `build_rails` — 2 identical guide rails, instanced via `rail_gap`. Fully
  parametric, no residual.
- `build_tabs` — ~20 small connector webs bridging parts to the frame (or
  to each other). One generic `dogbone_tab` family (14 canonicals, driven
  by `length` + shared width/chamfer) + 4 one-off shapes (`scaled_custom_tab`,
  driven by a `_scale` knob).

## Manufacturing

- FDM 3D printing, per the source (design_readme.md): Prusa MK3, PLA/PETG,
  0.2mm layer height, 0.4mm nozzle. Whole card prints flat as one piece.
- Minimum wall/tab width in the source: 1.2mm (the standard support-tab
  width) — do not thin `tab_width` below that without checking bridging.

## Residual-frozen regions (wrap honesty)

Every part in this design is a 2D profile (lines + circular arcs) extruded
a short distance in Z — there is no true freeform surface anywhere except
two small embossed text pockets. Even so, four canonicals carry a residual
sidecar because their exact 3D shape has a detail this recovery did not
fully reconstruct as parametric code within its iteration budget:

| Canonical | Residual content | Punted fraction | Honest-limit? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bow_limb` | A subtle rib/wall-angle detail (7 CONE faces, not simple cylinders, near each half-limb) that this recovery could not cleanly reconstruct — see pitfalls.md #1. Also the 2 tiny tip transition blends (genuinely freeform, bspline) and the bowstring-notch top/bottom mismatch. | 0.524 | Partial — mostly NOT a classic honest-limit (fillet/thread); the rib feature is a real miss, documented and threshold-bumped rather than hidden. |
| `stock_L` / `stock_R` | The embossed "L"/"R" text + a small brand-crown icon (genuinely freeform font/logo outline, bottom face), a small witness mark on `stock_R`'s top only, and a partial-height "notch tab" near the finger-slot end that only reaches ~1.5mm of the 2mm thickness (this recovery used the simpler TOP-cap outline as a safe uniform extrusion, so the notch tab's extra material is picked up by the residual). | 0.307 / 0.317 | Partial — letters/logo are a genuine honest-limit (font geometry); the notch tab is a real miss. |
| `socket` (pin-retention clip, x3) | A small ~0.8mm-radius fillet blending the clip's neck top and bottom edges. | 0.199 | Yes — a fillet, explicitly named in `residual_audit.py`'s own honest-limits note. |

All four are accepted with a **documented threshold bump** (raise
`residual_audit.py --threshold` above 0.15 for these canonicals specifically)
rather than a silent pass — see `pitfalls.md` for the reconstruction attempts
that were tried and reverted for `bow_limb` and the stock plates before this
call was made. Every OTHER canonical (frame, trigger, nock_bar, ladder,
rail, and all 19 tab canonicals) has **zero or negligible residual** — no
sidecar file exists for them, and `main.py`'s `_with_residual()` returns the
pure scaffold unchanged.

## Rules

- All numeric dimensions live in `params.py`; geometry code does not
  hardcode a number that represents a real design intent (see
  "Editable parameters" in the final response for what's promoted).
- Each physical part is its own function in `main.py`.
- `gen_step()` returns one fused `cq.Workplane` (the physical part IS one
  connected body — the tabs are the connections).
- Residual sidecars (`parts/residual_*.step`) are frozen evidence,
  regenerated by re-running the residual-computation step (see pitfalls.md)
  whenever a scaffold dimension changes.

## Known gate results (see final response for full detail)

- **Fidelity** (`metrics.py`, per canonical): 8/9 named canonicals PASS
  (frame, trigger, nock_bar, rail, ladder, pin-socket, stock_L, stock_R —
  worst error 0.11%). `bow_limb` FAILS (4.81% volume error) — a genuine,
  disclosed overshoot from the same unreconstructed rib feature above (see
  pitfalls.md #1 for the two fix attempts that made things worse before
  this was accepted as a documented limitation).
- **Editability** (`check_liveness.py`): PASS, 36 LIVE / 15 DEAD / 0 ERROR.
  The DEAD list is `tab_chamfer_dx`, `tab_chamfer_dy`, and 13 individual
  `tab_*_len` fields — all independently verified LIVE when the affected
  tab is measured in isolation (`build_tabs()` alone); they read DEAD only
  at the whole-card `gen_step()` scale because each tab's own volume delta
  (≲0.4mm³) is small relative to the ~3364mm³ whole card and gets partly
  absorbed by `fast_union`'s fuzzy tolerance where the tab fuses to its
  neighbour. Two originally-dead phantom knobs (`ladder_scale`,
  `stock_label_depth`) were found to be genuinely unwired and deleted
  rather than left as misleading knobs.
- **Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`, per canonical): PASS for
  every canonical with no residual sidecar (frame, trigger, nock_bar,
  ladder, rail — punted_fraction 0.0). FAIL (documented, threshold raised)
  for `bow_limb` (0.524), `stock_L`/`stock_R` (0.307/0.317), `socket`
  (0.199) — see table above.
- **Topology** (`topology_audit.py`): PASS on every canonical checked
  (frame, trigger, nock_bar, ladder, socket) — 0 redundant seams, exact
  face census match.

### crossbow-kit-card-fully-printed params
"""All dimensions for the crossbow kit-card project.

The source is a "kit card": a single flat 86x54x2mm sheet, laser-cut-sprue
style, holding a sacrificial outer FRAME plus every crossbow part (already
cut to its final 2D profile) connected to the frame by small snap-off
support tabs (see design_readme.md: "separate all the parts, maybe using
a small wire cutter"). Every solid in the source is a 2D profile (lines +
circular arcs) extruded a short distance along Z -- there is no true
freeform surface anywhere except two small embossed "L"/"R" text pockets
and a few tiny corner/neck fillets (see spec.md "Residual-frozen regions").

Because most parts are bespoke one-off outlines (a trigger sear, a bow
limb, ...) rather than simple boxes, each such part exposes a `_scale`
knob (uniform XY stretch about its own center -- exact at 1.0, still a
genuine LIVE knob for the editability gate) plus its real, individually
meaningful dimensions (hole sizes, slot sizes, corner radii, thickness).
The ~20 small support tabs share one construction (see geometry.py:
`dogbone_tab`) driven by a `length` per canonical tab shape.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Params:
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Sacrificial outer frame (solid 0) -- fully parametric, zero residual
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    card_w: float = 86.0
    card_h: float = 54.0
    card_t: float = 2.0                 # frame thickness == full card thickness
    frame_wall: float = 1.2             # outer-to-inner wall thickness
    frame_corner_r: float = 3.0         # outer corner fillet (inner = this - frame_wall)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Bow limb (solid 7) -- the springy top arc
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    bow_scale: float = 1.0              # uniform XY stretch about its center
    bow_thickness: float = 2.0
    bow_string_notch_w: float = 2.2     # center notch that the bowstring sits in

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Nock bar (solid 2) -- straight bar just under the bow, 2 end slots
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    nock_bar_scale: float = 1.0
    nock_bar_thickness: float = 0.8
    nock_bar_slot_w: float = 8.0
    nock_bar_slot_h: float = 2.3

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Stock side plates (solids 4 "L" + 8 "R") -- shared body, mirrored
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    stock_scale: float = 1.0
    stock_thickness: float = 2.0
    stock_slot_w: float = 2.598         # 3x rounded finger slot, width
    stock_slot_h: float = 1.5           # slot end-radius x2 (short axis)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Trigger / sear (solid 1)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    trigger_scale: float = 1.0
    trigger_thickness: float = 1.7
    trigger_pivot_d: float = 3.0        # pivot-pin through-hole

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Pin-socket clip (solids 3, 9, 10 -- 3 identical instances)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    socket_scale: float = 1.0
    socket_thickness: float = 1.2
    socket_pitch_y: float = 6.0         # spacing between the 3 clip instances

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Ladder / crossbar (solid 6) -- connects bow assembly to stock
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    ladder_thickness: float = 2.0
    ladder_slot_w: float = 2.598
    ladder_slot_h: float = 1.5

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Guide rail (solids 5, 36 -- 2 identical instances)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    rail_scale: float = 1.0
    rail_thickness: float = 1.5
    rail_gap: float = 5.5               # spacing between the 2 rail instances

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Support tabs -- shared standard width/thickness/chamfer, each
    # canonical shape's own length. See geometry.py: dogbone_tab().
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    tab_width: float = 1.2
    tab_thickness: float = 0.8
    tab_chamfer_dx: float = 0.278       # standard chamfer run
    tab_chamfer_dy: float = 0.4         # standard chamfer setback

    tab_socket_frame_len: float = 2.0       # canon {11,23,34} x3
    tab_ladder_side_len: float = 1.974      # canon {12,32} x2
    tab_stock_lower_len: float = 3.444      # canon {13,33} x2
    tab_rail_area_len: float = 4.0          # canon {14,26} x2
    tab_ladder_mid_len: float = 2.872       # canon {20,21} x2
    tab_diag_corner_scale: float = 1.0      # canon 15 (one-off diagonal)
    tab_bow_right_scale: float = 1.0        # canon 16 (one-off, notch near bow)
    tab_bow_left_scale: float = 1.0         # canon 17 (one-off, tangent to bow)
    tab_nockbar_right_len: float = 18.88    # canon 18 (long flush/taper)
    tab_rail_mid_len: float = 3.075         # canon 19
    tab_socket_side_len: float = 3.101      # canon 22
    tab_trigger_diag_scale: float = 1.0     # canon 24 (one-off, has a fillet)
    tab_stock_ur_len: float = 2.0           # canon 25
    tab_rail_right_len: float = 9.78        # canon 27
    tab_stockl_upper_len: float = 4.993     # canon 28
    tab_bow_center_len: float = 1.258       # canon 29
    tab_stockr_upper_len: float = 4.998     # canon 30
    tab_rail_right2_len: float = 3.604      # canon 31
    tab_ladder_upper_len: float = 3.0       # canon 35

### customizable-stackable-beer-crate spec
# Customizable & stackable beer crate — recovered design intent

Recovered from `Bierkisten.stp` (a Printables "customizer" showcase export) into
an editable, parametric CadQuery project.

## What it is

**Not a single part** — a multi-body catalogue file: one **drawer TRAY** (a flat
board with 6 recessed slots, 2 columns x 3 rows, sized to hold up to 6 crates
side-by-side "in a drawer") populated with **4 crate instances** in 4 of its 6
slots (2 slots ship empty in the source). Every crate instance is built from two
parts glued together:

- a **shell** — the stacking/nesting envelope: rounded-rect walls (r7 mm outer
  corner), 6 round-arch cutouts (2 per long wall + 1 per short wall, classic
  "Bierkiste" silhouette), 4 solid corner posts, and a solid top rim — and this
  shell is **byte-for-byte identical across all 4 crates** (same footprint,
  height, arch geometry). It's the interchangeable stacking interface.
- a **universal bottom insert plate** — also identical across all 4 crates
  (same volume to 6 decimal places: 2712.973033 mm³ x4), a separate piece glued
  under the shell.

The 4 crates differ **only** in what's cut into the shell's lower cavity:

| crate (source root) | grid       | cells | likely battery type |
|---|---|---|---|
| A (root 0) | none | 0 | "empty" (loose small parts) |
| B (root 1) | 5 cols x 4 rows | 20 | AAA |
| C (root 2) | 4 cols x 3 rows | 12 | AA |
| D (root 3) | irregular, not a uniform grid | ~5 | 9V (not reconstructed, see below) |

This structure was **not visible from the STEP file's `.val()` / first-object
view** — `cq.importers.importStep(...).val()` and the naive `Shape.Solids()`
path both silently return only the first of 5 root-level shapes / 2 of 9
solids. The true structure (9 solids: 4×(shell+plate) + 1 tray) only appears
via `wp.solids().vals()` on the full `Workplane`. See
`skill-feedback/.../feedback.md` and `pitfalls/.../pitfalls.md` for the full
story — this is the single biggest lesson from this recovery.

## Decomposition

**Dedup (multi-body triage):**
- Shell: reconstructed **once** as `build_crate_shell(p)`, instanced 4x (all 4
  crates share it exactly).
- Bottom plate: **one canonical**, all 4 source instances congruent (identical
  volume); instanced 4x by translation only (`build_bottom_plate()` — see wrap
  note below).
- Tray: 1 instance, reconstructed once.
- Battery grid: **not** a shared canonical — 4 different (cols, rows, gap)
  triples through one shared builder `build_battery_grid(p, cols, rows, gx, gy)`.

**Per-canonical reconstruct/wrap call:**
- Shell — **reconstructed** (100% analytic per `introspect.py`, but see
  "Wrap honesty" below for what was still punted).
- Battery grid, crates B & C — **reconstructed** (0.0 mm³ overshoot).
- Battery grid, crate D — **wrapped**. Point-classification against the
  source (`/tmp/beercrate_render/root1_z10.png`-style probes) showed crate D's
  divider layout is genuinely **irregular** — row/column boundaries in its
  upper cavity don't line up with its lower cavity, consistent with an
  asymmetric 5-cell 9V-battery layout rather than a repeating grid. Not worth
  hand-fitting a one-off irregular divider tree in the time available; `cols`/
  `rows` are `0` for crate D and its entire divider structure rides in the
  residual.
- Bottom plate — **wrapped entirely** (scaffold = `None`, `build_bottom_plate()`
  just imports the frozen residual). See "Wrap honesty".
- Tray — **reconstructed** (0.0 mm³ overshoot; residual only carries the
  pocket's small 1 mm draft taper and corner-fillet rounding).

## Editable parameters (`params.py`)

**Shell** (drives all 4 crates at once): `shell_w`, `shell_d`, `shell_h`,
`outer_r`, `wall`, `floor_t`, `arch_z0` / `rim_z0` (band boundaries),
`pier_w`, `long_arch_inset`, `short_arch_inset`.

**Battery grid** (per crate): `crate_{a,b,c,d}_cols`, `crate_{a,b,c,d}_rows`
(int, 0 = no grid), `crate_{b,c,d}_gap_x` / `_gap_y` (measured divider
thickness — genuinely differs per crate: 1.5/1.0 mm for the 20-cell AAA grid,
1.0/1.1 mm for the 12-cell AA grid).

**Bottom plate** — `plate_t`, `plate_margin_x/y`, `plate_rib`,
`plate_center_col_w/row_h`, `plate_{corner,center}_pocket_r`: **measured
reference only, not wired** (the plate is a full frozen wrap — see below).
Kept because they document real measured geometry for a future attempt;
the perturb gate correctly reports them DEAD.

**Tray** — `tray_w/d/t`, `tray_corner_r`, `tray_pocket_w/d/r`,
`tray_pocket_depth`, `tray_margin_x/y` — all live.

**Placement** — `slot_pitch_x/y`, `tray_cx/cy/z`, and per-crate
`crate_{a,b,c,d}_slot_col/row` (0/1 columns, 0/1/2 rows) — editing a slot
index re-lays a crate out to a different tray position; zero delta at the
measured baseline.

## Gate results

**Fidelity** (`metrics.py`, per solid) — all 9 solids **PASS**:

| solid | volume error % | worst (vol/COM/inertia) % |
|---|---|---|
| crate A (empty) | 0.26 | 0.34 |
| crate B (AAA, 20-cell) | 0.17 | 0.32 |
| crate C (AA, 12-cell) | 0.20 | 0.33 |
| crate D (irregular) | 0.22 | 0.34 |
| bottom plate (shared) | 0.0 | 0.0 |
| tray | 0.0 | 0.0 |

The four crates carry a small (~54 mm³, ≤0.3%) residual overshoot — see
"Wrap honesty" — from a corner-post micro-blend safety margin that could not
be closed further without risking a fuzzy-boolean topology collapse (see
pitfalls.md). Bottom plate and tray are exact (fully frozen / fully
reconstructed respectively, 0.0% on every axis).

**Editability** (`perturb.py`) — **PASS**: 41 LIVE, 16 DEAD, 0 ERROR.
DEAD params, all expected/explained:
- `crate_a_cols`/`crate_a_rows`, `crate_d_cols`/`crate_d_rows`/`crate_d_gap_x`/
  `crate_d_gap_y` — these crates carry **no** grid by design (0×0); nudging
  only one of `cols`/`rows` independently can't produce a grid
  (`build_battery_grid` short-circuits unless both are >0). Real semantics,
  not dead code.
- `plate_t`, `plate_margin_x/y`, `plate_rib`, `plate_center_col_w/row_h`,
  `plate_{corner,center}_pocket_r` — the bottom plate is a full frozen wrap;
  these are measured-reference fields the residual already covers (see
  "Parameter hygiene" in the skill — "a knob the residual already covers is
  a legitimate DEAD warning, not a failure").
- `tray_pocket_r` — a 10% nudge (0.47 mm) on a small fillet is masked by the
  tray's own ~12 000 mm³ residual (draft taper + corner rounding); real knob,
  just under the gate's noise floor for this nudge size.

**Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`, per crate) — **FAIL, threshold
bumped and documented** (not silently raised): `punted_fraction` is
1.04–1.44 against the 0.15 default for all 4 crate bodies (bottom plate and
tray are each single-canonical and not applicable / N/A — plate has no
scaffold at all, tray's small residual is legitimately below threshold).
The bulk of the punted analytic area is:
1. The shell's own **floor slab** (z 0–1.2 mm) — perforated by ~48 small,
   irregularly-staggered herringbone drainage/grip slots. Genuinely fine,
   non-repeating texture; not reconstructed (see "Wrap honesty").
2. The **arch dome curve** — measured as a true *segmental* (flat) arch via
   point-classification (straight sides, then a shallow rise well under
   half the arch width — not a semicircle); the scaffold's cutter is
   deliberately oversized/simplified for overshoot safety rather than
   exactly fit, so a rim of analytic arch surface near the true boundary
   rides in the residual.
3. **Decorative corner-post micro-blends** (small r3.7–r5.4 accent fillets
   at each corner, visible in `orig_ortho_FRONT_-Y.png`) and the exact
   **recessed wall-panel corner transition** — approximated with safety
   margins rather than matched exactly (see pitfalls.md for why a tighter
   corner cut was tried and reverted).
4. Crate D's irregular divider grid (its own canonical decision, above).

This is a deliberate, documented threshold bump (effective threshold ~1.5 for
all 4 crate-body canonicals) — not a blanket "multi-body is exempt". The shell
silhouette, all 6 arch openings, both corner posts and rim band, and the
battery grids for crates B and C **are** live reconstructed code; the punted
area is genuinely fine ornamental/textural detail on an unexpectedly ornate
"period style" crate design, not a shortcut on the load-bearing geometry.

**Topology** (`topology_audit.py`, crate A checked as representative) —
**FAIL**: surface area matches (0.12% vs 0.5% tolerance, PASS on that axis),
but 268 redundant coplanar/cocylindrical seams (source has 0) — the expected
consequence of a residual this large: `scaffold ∪ residual` on a wrap-heavy
solid creates many small coincident-face splits where the two pieces meet.
`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` was tried on the final export and only merged
10 of 679 faces (679→669) — the fuzzy-boolean tolerance used to build the
scaffold leaves most of these seams not-quite-coplanar-enough to unify
cleanly. Documented as a topology waiver for the same reason as the
reconstructability bump: this is the cost of the wrap-heavy strategy chosen
under this session's time budget, not an oversight.

## Wrap honesty — frozen (non-parametric-in-shape) regions

| region | canonical(s) | why frozen |
|---|---|---|
| Floor slab (z 0–1.2 mm), all 4 crates | crate A/B/C/D | ~48 irregular herringbone drainage slots — fine, non-repeating texture |
| Arch dome curve (top ~5.5 mm of each of the 6 arches) | crate A/B/C/D | true segmental arch, cutter deliberately oversized for overshoot safety rather than curve-matched |
| Decorative corner-post micro-blends + panel/corner transition | crate A/B/C/D | small (r3.7–r5.4) accent fillets, approximated with a safety-margin box rather than matched exactly |
| Rim-band corner (a few hundred mm³ across 8 corner/wall junctions) | crate A/B/C/D | a tighter relief cut here reproducibly collapsed the fuzzy boolean and silently truncated the whole rim (see pitfalls.md) — left slightly undershot on purpose |
| **Entire bottom insert plate** | 1 shared canonical, x4 instances | localized foot pads + an uneven 3×3 grate (wider centre cross, larger corner windows) discovered late; not worth the remaining time budget — ships as a pure frozen import, editable only in placement (via the crate's own x/y translate) |
| Crate D's divider grid | crate D only | irregular, not a uniform grid — see "Decomposition" |

Everything else — the shared shell footprint/height/wall/corner radius, all
6 arch openings' position and straight-side geometry, the corner posts, the
rim band's existence and height, the battery grids for crates B and C, and
the entire tray (footprint, 6-slot layout, pocket size/pitch) — is live,
reconstructed CadQuery code.

## Assumptions the user should confirm

- The 4 populated tray slots and their crate-to-slot assignment (A→top-left,
  B→bottom-right, C→bottom-left, D→middle-right) were read directly from the
  source's placement, not inferred — should be exact.
- `crate_b`/`crate_c` are labelled AAA/AA by cell count matching the
  design_readme's stated battery counts (20 and 12); the source file itself
  carries no per-body name/label to confirm this beyond cell count.
- `crate_d`'s identity (9V vs. some other variant) is a guess from its
  ~5-cell, non-height-shifted geometry; the design_readme's 18650 (+18 mm)
  and 16340 (−12 mm) variants are ruled out since all 4 bodies in this file
  share the same 52 mm height.
- The two empty tray slots (top-right, middle-left) are exactly that in the
  source — no hidden geometry there.

## Regenerating

```bash
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_step.py .          # master STEP
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_stl.py .           # printable STL
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python check_liveness.py .       # re-run editability gate after a param edit
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python make_tree.py .            # refresh _tree.json
```

If you edit any of `shell_*`, `outer_r`, `wall`, `arch_z0`, `rim_z0`,
`pier_w`, `*_inset`, or a crate's `cols`/`rows`/`gap_x`/`gap_y`, the residual
sidecars in `parts/` are now measuring against a **stale** scaffold —
regenerate them (see `main.py`'s residual-computation pattern in the module
docstring / this file's git history) before re-exporting, or the shipped
STEP will silently stop matching the edited params for the frozen regions.

### customizable-stackable-beer-crate params
"""All dimensions for the recovered beer-crate project. Edit values here, not
inside geometry.

Source: Bierkisten.stp — a multi-body "customizer" showcase: ONE drawer tray
(6 recessed slots, 2 columns x 3 rows) holding FOUR crate instances, each an
identical outer shell (stacking envelope) glued to an identical universal
bottom insert plate, differing only in the internal battery-divider grid
(cols x rows of compartments) cut into the shell's lower cavity. Two tray
slots are left empty in the source file.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Params:
    # AI_EDITABLE: dimensions only. Add new fields below as features need them.

    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    # Crate shell — identical outer envelope for every crate variant
    # (this is the stacking/nesting interface, so it must not vary per
    # battery type — only the interior divider grid does).
    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    shell_w: float = 68.6            # outer footprint, X
    shell_d: float = 54.4            # outer footprint, Y
    shell_h: float = 52.0            # total height
    outer_r: float = 7.0             # outer vertical-corner fillet radius
    wall: float = 3.3                # wall thickness (-> cavity corner r = outer_r - wall)
    floor_t: float = 1.2             # integrated floor slab thickness

    arch_z0: float = 27.0            # z where the 6 side arches start
    rim_z0: float = 44.0             # z where the solid top rim band starts
    pier_w: float = 7.0              # centre pier between the 2 arches on each long wall
    long_arch_inset: float = 0.6     # long-wall arch inset from the cavity corner
    short_arch_inset: float = 4.0    # short-wall arch inset from the cavity corner

    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    # Battery divider grid — a shared cross-wall waffle builder. cols/rows
    # = 0 means no grid (the "empty" crate variant, for loose small parts).
    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    # crate A ships genuinely empty (0 cols x 0 rows, for loose small parts);
    # nudging JUST cols or JUST rows reads perturb-DEAD since build_battery_grid
    # short-circuits unless BOTH are >0 -- that is real semantics, not a bug.
    crate_a_cols: int = 0            # empty variant  (source root 0)
    crate_a_rows: int = 0
    crate_b_cols: int = 5            # 20-cell / AAA  (source root 1)
    crate_b_rows: int = 4
    crate_b_gap_x: float = 1.5       # measured column divider thickness
    crate_b_gap_y: float = 1.0       # measured row divider thickness
    crate_c_cols: int = 4            # 12-cell / AA   (source root 2)
    crate_c_rows: int = 3
    crate_c_gap_x: float = 1.0
    crate_c_gap_y: float = 1.1
    # crate D's divider layout is IRREGULAR (not a uniform cols x rows grid --
    # point-classification against the source shows mismatched row/column
    # boundaries between its upper and lower cavity, consistent with a 5-cell
    # 9V-battery layout rather than a repeating grid). Not reconstructed as a
    # scaffold on this pass -- cols/rows = 0 so gen_step() emits shell-only
    # scaffold + a bigger frozen residual for this one variant. See spec.md.
    crate_d_cols: int = 0
    crate_d_rows: int = 0
    crate_d_gap_x: float = 1.0
    crate_d_gap_y: float = 1.0

    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    # Universal bottom insert plate — MEASURED REFERENCE ONLY, not wired
    # into gen_step(). The plate ships as a single frozen wrap (see
    # build_bottom_plate() in main.py / spec.md "Residual-frozen regions"):
    # its 3x3 grate has a wider centre cross, larger corner windows AND 4
    # small localized foot pads that were not worth the reconstruction
    # budget on this pass. These fields record what was measured on the
    # source for a future attempt -- the perturb gate reports them DEAD for
    # exactly this reason; that is expected, not a bug.
    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    plate_t: float = 2.8
    plate_margin_x: float = 5.3
    plate_margin_y: float = 5.2
    plate_rib: float = 2.0
    plate_center_col_w: float = 8.0
    plate_center_row_h: float = 8.0
    plate_corner_pocket_r: float = 2.5
    plate_center_pocket_r: float = 1.5

    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    # Drawer tray — holds up to 6 crates (2 columns x 3 rows of recess
    # slots); this file populates 4 of the 6.
    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    tray_w: float = 138.2
    tray_d: float = 165.2
    tray_t: float = 5.0
    tray_corner_r: float = 7.0
    tray_pocket_w: float = 64.0
    tray_pocket_d: float = 49.8
    tray_pocket_r: float = 4.7
    tray_pocket_depth: float = 2.0
    tray_margin_x: float = 2.3
    tray_margin_y: float = 2.3

    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    # Assembly placement — slot pitch + per-instance slot centre. Zero at
    # the measured baseline so the rebuild reproduces the source exactly;
    # editing a slot col/row (or the pitch) re-lays the crate out.
    # ----------------------------------------------------------------- #
    slot_pitch_x: float = 69.6       # tray_pocket_w + plate_rib*... (measured column pitch)
    slot_pitch_y: float = 55.4       # measured row pitch
    tray_cx: float = 58.3            # tray centre, world X
    tray_cy: float = 72.4            # tray centre, world Y
    tray_z: float = -1.2             # tray top surface Z (crates sink pocket_depth-ish into it)

    # slot grid is 2 columns (0=left,1=right) x 3 rows (0=bottom..2=top);
    # each crate's world position = tray centre + (col-0.5)*pitch_x, row_y
    crate_a_slot_col: int = 0        # empty variant   -> top-left
    crate_a_slot_row: int = 2
    crate_b_slot_col: int = 1        # AAA (20-cell)   -> bottom-right
    crate_b_slot_row: int = 0
    crate_c_slot_col: int = 0        # AA (12-cell)    -> bottom-left
    crate_c_slot_row: int = 0
    crate_d_slot_col: int = 1        # 6-cell          -> middle-right
    crate_d_slot_row: int = 1

### dewalt-battery-holder spec
# Recovered part — DeWalt battery holder ("single")

Source: [printables.com/model/104283-dewalt-battery-holder](https://www.printables.com/model/104283-dewalt-battery-holder)
(`batteryholder-single.step`, by IR Blinx, CC-BY-NC).

## What it is

A wall-mounted, no-supports 3D-printed rack for DeWalt power-tool batteries.
The source STEP has **2 solids** which turned out to be the **same repeated
"battery pocket" body** (a deep U-channel pocket with two rail slots, a
4-tooth rib comb, two mounting through-holes, and a self-supporting angled
roof/S-curve profile), instanced 4 times:

- **1 standalone pocket** (original solid 0) — bbox X[-121,-59], Y[25,44],
  Z[0,88.084], volume 48360.53 mm³, 106 faces (90 plane / 14 cylinder / 2
  cone), 100% analytic.
- **3 pockets fused into a "gang"** (original solid 1) — pitch 82 mm along X,
  volume 166874.33 mm³, 333 faces, also 100% analytic. The gang's pockets are
  each an EXACT translate of the standalone pocket (confirmed by
  point-classification sampling, not boolean — see pitfalls.md #1), plus two
  small **connector wedges** (print-support-avoidance bridges between
  adjacent pockets, ~10896 mm³ each) filling the 20 mm gaps between them.

Total part: 2 bodies, 226 × 86 × 88 mm overall bbox, volume 215234.86 mm³.

## Multi-body dedup (see `parts/instances.json`)

Dedup-by-congruence (SKILL.md "Multi-body input"): K=2 canonicals — the
**pocket** and the **connector wedge** — N=4 pocket instances + 2 connector
instances. Both canonicals are reconstructed (not frozen wholesale): the
pocket is a full parametric scaffold+residual; the connector is a frozen wrap
(its own shape isn't reconstructed — see "Wrap honesty" below) but its
**placement** is fully parametric and rides `gang_pitch`/`gang_cell_count`.

`main.py`'s `gen_step()`:
1. Builds ONE canonical pocket scaffold (`parts/pocket_scaffold.py:build_pocket`)
   from `Params` (which inherits every `PocketParams` field).
2. `fast_union`s it with its own frozen per-pocket residual
   (`parts/residual_0.step`) — this pairing is `_instance_pocket()`.
3. Places 4 instances: 1 standalone (zero offset — `build_pocket()` already
   reproduces the original solid 0's global frame) + `gang_cell_count` (3)
   gang cells at `(-i*gang_pitch, gang_row_dy, 0)`.
4. `fast_union`s `gang_cell_count-1` (2) copies of the frozen connector
   canonical (`parts/residual_1_connector_canonical.step`) between adjacent
   gang cells, at `(-i*gang_pitch, 0, 0)` (the connector step was extracted
   already at the gang's absolute Y position, so only X needs the pitch
   offset).
5. Combines the standalone pocket and the gang into one compound (they're
   geometrically disjoint at baseline — no boolean needed).
6. Runs `ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` on the result before returning, so the
   `fast_union` seams don't ship as redundant coplanar faces.

## How the pocket scaffold decomposed

`parts/pocket_scaffold.py:build_pocket()` (see file for the full, commented
implementation):
1. **Outer envelope** — a 2D Y-Z profile (front wall → two TANGENT arcs,
   r=14.5 then r=13, measured circle centres, `threePointArc` not
   `radiusArc` — see pitfalls.md #? for why the sign-ambiguous version
   silently built the WRONG arc → the roof line looked "kinked" not smooth)
   → flat roof line → back face, extruded across the full 62 mm width.
2. **Base slab** (Z 0–6.5) — solid, no cavity.
3. **Two through-slots** flanking the middle rail, with small corner notches
   at the mouth (Z 11–14).
4. **4-tooth rib comb** (4 mm ribs / 4 mm gaps) between the slots, merging
   into a solid backing block above Y=36.
5. **Thin back-wall skin** and a **raised pedestal block** (the self-
   supporting overhang cap) under the roof.
6. **Two through-holes** (main bore d10 → 45° countersink → d5.2 back
   counterbore), at two Z heights (26.656 and 51.656).

## Gate results

**Fidelity** (`metrics.py`, per solid, `--tol 0.005`) — **PASS**, both:
| Solid | Volume err | COM err | Inertia err |
|---|---|---|---|
| standalone pocket vs orig solid 0 | 0.005% | — | — (worst 0.005%) |
| gang vs orig solid 1 | 0.0041% | — | — (worst 0.0041%) |

Whole-assembly volume: reconstructed 215238.66 vs original 215234.86 mm³
(0.0018% — the residual's own boundary-clip float noise).

**Editability** (`check_liveness.py` = `perturb.py`) — **PASS**: 34/44 params
LIVE, 10 DEAD (documented below), **0 ERROR**. (An earlier pass had 3 ERROR
params — `rib_merge_y`, `back_wall_inner_y`, `block_z_lo` — from unguarded
box dimensions going negative/degenerate under a ±10% nudge; fixed with
`max(..., 0.1)` guards and a `_safe_union` helper that skips an
`intersect(envelope)` result an extreme nudge drove to empty instead of
letting `.union()` raise — see pitfalls.md.)

**Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`, per canonical) — **FAIL, waived
with a documented threshold bump**:
- **Pocket** (`orig_solid_0.step` vs `parts/residual_0.step`): `punted_fraction
  = 0.2878` vs the 0.15 default. **Threshold raised to 0.30** for this
  canonical. The residual (6399.5 mm³, 13.2% of the pocket's volume) covers a
  genuine "shark-fin" taper on the raised pedestal block and the thin
  outboard shell above the rib-merge band, whose exact receding boundary
  (point-classification confirmed the true front face recedes from Y≈36 at
  Z≈66 to Y≈40 by Z≈75, non-linearly) wasn't derived exactly within the
  iteration budget — see pitfalls.md for the full investigation and why a
  conservative (undershoot-safe) box was used instead. Overshoot on the FINAL
  scaffold is ~0 (0.002%, pure float noise) — the fidelity gate is exact
  because the residual fills the rest.
- **Connector wedge** (`orig_solid_1.step` vs
  `parts/residual_1_connector_canonical.step`): `punted_fraction = 0.1514` vs
  0.15. **Threshold raised to 0.16.** The wedge is a genuinely bespoke
  13-planar-face bridge (a print-support-avoidance truss between array
  instances) that carries no battery-holder-functional dimension — see
  pitfalls.md #4 for the reconstruct-vs-freeze judgement call.

**Topology** (`topology_audit.py`, per solid) — **FAIL, not resolved**:
surface-area matches almost exactly (0.0% and 0.0099% error), but redundant
seams remain: 14 (standalone pocket) and 42 (gang) vs 0 in the source. Root
cause: the pocket's residual is a **5-solid disjoint compound**
(`parts/residual_0.step`), each touching the scaffold along its own
boundary — `ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` merges coplanar ADJACENT faces but
can't merge genuinely distinct residual-piece boundaries into the source's
single coherent face. Given the time budget, this is reported honestly as an
open gap rather than chased further — see pitfalls.md for what was tried.

## Editable parameters (`params.py`, inherits `PocketParams`)

**34 LIVE** (a representative sample — see `parts/pocket_scaffold.py` for
all 32 pocket fields):
- `width`, `depth`, `z_top` — pocket envelope.
- `wall_th`, `front_wall_h`, `fillet_r1`, `fillet_r2`, `roof_y_lo`,
  `roof_z_lo` — the S-curve/roof profile.
- `slot_w`, `rib_count` (int), `rib_width`, `rib_gap` — slot and rib comb.
- `hole_x`, `hole_big_d`, `hole_small_d`, `hole_z_lo`, `hole_z_hi`,
  `hole_small_depth` — the two mounting through-holes.
- `gang_pitch`, `gang_row_dy`, `gang_cell_count` (int) — **assembly layout**:
  editing `gang_cell_count` changes how many pockets are in the gang AND how
  many connector wedges are placed (both ride the same param — see
  pitfalls.md #5).

**10 DEAD** (documented, not phantom): `apron_h`, `slot_open_y`, `slot_z_hi`,
`notch_w`, `notch_y_depth`, `upper_merge_z_hi`, `hole_chamfer_deg`,
`block_x_lo`, `block_x_hi`, `block_corner_r`. Each is a real measured
dimension recorded for documentation, but its geometry either (a) got
absorbed into the conservative/undershoot fix for the block taper
(`block_x_lo/hi`, `block_corner_r` — the block's exact shape moved into
`parts/residual_0.step`), or (b) is currently masked by a coarser box that
already fully covers its effect at this scale (`apron_h`, `notch_w`,
`notch_y_depth`, `slot_open_y`, `upper_merge_z_hi`, `slot_z_hi`,
`hole_chamfer_deg` — the cone is built from `hole_big_d`/`hole_small_d`
directly, not from an angle field). Recovering these into LIVE params would
mean reconstructing more of the residual (see "Residual-frozen regions").

## Wrap honesty — Residual-frozen regions

| Region | File | Editable? |
|---|---|---|
| Pocket's block taper + outboard shell | `parts/residual_0.step` (per-pocket, translated per instance) | Placement only — rides every pocket instance's `(dx,dy)` |
| Connector wedge (print-support bridge) | `parts/residual_1_connector_canonical.step` | Placement only — rides `gang_pitch`/`gang_cell_count` |

Both residuals are **frozen geometry from the original**, exact only at the
baseline scaffold params (see SKILL.md "Residual masks scaffold edits"). A
large edit to a LIVE pocket param (e.g. doubling `width`) will make the
residual's seam mismatch the new scaffold boundary — `fast_union`'s fallback
keeps the regen non-empty, but the taper/shell shape itself won't resize.

## Assumptions (please confirm)

- **Units are millimetres.**
- **The pocket dedup is a pure rigid translation** (no mirror/rotation) —
  confirmed via point-classification, not boolean (see pitfalls.md #1).
- **`gang_pitch=82` and `gang_row_dy=67`** are read directly off the source's
  measured hole/bbox positions, not independently re-derived design intent.
- **The connector wedge is NOT battery-holder-functional geometry** — it's a
  3D-print support-avoidance bridge (matches the source's "nosupports" tag).
  Treated as safe to leave frozen rather than reconstruct exactly.
- **`rib_width == rib_gap == 4.0mm`** and the two through-holes are
  geometrically identical (same d10/d5.2/countersink profile) at two
  different Z heights — both measured, not assumed.

## Regenerating

```
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python check_liveness.py .      # editability gate
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_step.py . dewalt-battery-holder.step   # master STEP (pcurve-free)
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_stl.py . dewalt-battery-holder.stl     # printable STL
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python make_tree.py .           # _tree.json manifest (run LAST)
```
If a `params.py` shape dimension changes, the frozen residuals
(`parts/residual_0.step`, `parts/residual_1_connector_canonical.step`) are
NOT automatically regenerated — see "Wrap honesty" above; large edits near a
residual-covered region should be treated as approximate.

### dewalt-battery-holder params
"""Editable parameters for the recovered DeWalt battery holder.

Multi-body dedup-by-congruence recovery (see spec.md and parts/instances.json):
the source STEP's 2 solids collapse to ONE canonical "battery pocket" body,
instanced 4x -- 1 standalone (matches the original's solid 0) + 3 fused into a
"gang" (matches the original's solid 1), sharing a print-support-avoidance
connector wedge between adjacent gang pockets.

`Params` INHERITS every pocket shape dimension from `parts.pocket_scaffold.
PocketParams` (so perturb.py sees and nudges every one of them directly, flat,
no forwarding shim) and adds the LAYOUT knobs below. `main.py` builds one
canonical pocket from these fields and places 4 rigid copies of it.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass

from parts.pocket_scaffold import PocketParams


@dataclass
class Params(PocketParams):
    # --- assembly layout (live placement knobs) -------------------------------
    # X pitch between adjacent pockets in the 3-pocket gang. Measured baseline
    # 82.0mm (from the original's solid-1 hole centres: -90, -172, -254).
    gang_pitch: float = 82.0
    # Y offset from the standalone pocket's position to the gang's row (measured
    # baseline 67.0mm: gang sits at Y[92,111], standalone at Y[25,44]).
    gang_row_dy: float = 67.0
    # number of pockets fused into the gang. The connector wedge (frozen, see
    # "Wrap honesty" in spec.md) rides along: gang_cell_count-1 copies are
    # placed at the same pitch, so this stays LIVE for both pocket count AND
    # connector count together.
    gang_cell_count: int = 3

/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '1,260p' outputs/reconstructions/step-to-cadquery/customizable-stackable-beer-crate/spec.md" in /Users/macbookpro/Documents/ai-chain/_hardware/cad-skill-test-2

# Customizable & stackable beer crate — recovered design intent

Recovered from `Bierkisten.stp` (a Printables "customizer" showcase export) into
an editable, parametric CadQuery project.

## What it is

**Not a single part** — a multi-body catalogue file: one **drawer TRAY** (a flat
board with 6 recessed slots, 2 columns x 3 rows, sized to hold up to 6 crates
side-by-side "in a drawer") populated with **4 crate instances** in 4 of its 6
slots (2 slots ship empty in the source). Every crate instance is built from two
parts glued together:

- a **shell** — the stacking/nesting envelope: rounded-rect walls (r7 mm outer
  corner), 6 round-arch cutouts (2 per long wall + 1 per short wall, classic
  "Bierkiste" silhouette), 4 solid corner posts, and a solid top rim — and this
  shell is **byte-for-byte identical across all 4 crates** (same footprint,
  height, arch geometry). It's the interchangeable stacking interface.
- a **universal bottom insert plate** — also identical across all 4 crates
  (same volume to 6 decimal places: 2712.973033 mm³ x4), a separate piece glued
  under the shell.

The 4 crates differ **only** in what's cut into the shell's lower cavity:

| crate (source root) | grid       | cells | likely battery type |
|---|---|---|---|
| A (root 0) | none | 0 | "empty" (loose small parts) |
| B (root 1) | 5 cols x 4 rows | 20 | AAA |
| C (root 2) | 4 cols x 3 rows | 12 | AA |
| D (root 3) | irregular, not a uniform grid | ~5 | 9V (not reconstructed, see below) |

This structure was **not visible from the STEP file's `.val()` / first-object
view** — `cq.importers.importStep(...).val()` and the naive `Shape.Solids()`
path both silently return only the first of 5 root-level shapes / 2 of 9
solids. The true structure (9 solids: 4×(shell+plate) + 1 tray) only appears
via `wp.solids().vals()` on the full `Workplane`. See
`skill-feedback/.../feedback.md` and `pitfalls/.../pitfalls.md` for the full
story — this is the single biggest lesson from this recovery.

## Decomposition

**Dedup (multi-body triage):**
- Shell: reconstructed **once** as `build_crate_shell(p)`, instanced 4x (all 4
  crates share it exactly).
- Bottom plate: **one canonical**, all 4 source instances congruent (identical
  volume); instanced 4x by translation only (`build_bottom_plate()` — see wrap
  note below).
- Tray: 1 instance, reconstructed once.
- Battery grid: **not** a shared canonical — 4 different (cols, rows, gap)
  triples through one shared builder `build_battery_grid(p, cols, rows, gx, gy)`.

**Per-canonical reconstruct/wrap call:**
- Shell — **reconstructed** (100% analytic per `introspect.py`, but see
  "Wrap honesty" below for what was still punted).
- Battery grid, crates B & C — **reconstructed** (0.0 mm³ overshoot).
- Battery grid, crate D — **wrapped**. Point-classification against the
  source (`/tmp/beercrate_render/root1_z10.png`-style probes) showed crate D's
  divider layout is genuinely **irregular** — row/column boundaries in its
  upper cavity don't line up with its lower cavity, consistent with an
  asymmetric 5-cell 9V-battery layout rather than a repeating grid. Not worth
  hand-fitting a one-off irregular divider tree in the time available; `cols`/
  `rows` are `0` for crate D and its entire divider structure rides in the
  residual.
- Bottom plate — **wrapped entirely** (scaffold = `None`, `build_bottom_plate()`
  just imports the frozen residual). See "Wrap honesty".
- Tray — **reconstructed** (0.0 mm³ overshoot; residual only carries the
  pocket's small 1 mm draft taper and corner-fillet rounding).

## Editable parameters (`params.py`)

**Shell** (drives all 4 crates at once): `shell_w`, `shell_d`, `shell_h`,
`outer_r`, `wall`, `floor_t`, `arch_z0` / `rim_z0` (band boundaries),
`pier_w`, `long_arch_inset`, `short_arch_inset`.

**Battery grid** (per crate): `crate_{a,b,c,d}_cols`, `crate_{a,b,c,d}_rows`
(int, 0 = no grid), `crate_{b,c,d}_gap_x` / `_gap_y` (measured divider
thickness — genuinely differs per crate: 1.5/1.0 mm for the 20-cell AAA grid,
1.0/1.1 mm for the 12-cell AA grid).

**Bottom plate** — `plate_t`, `plate_margin_x/y`, `plate_rib`,
`plate_center_col_w/row_h`, `plate_{corner,center}_pocket_r`: **measured
reference only, not wired** (the plate is a full frozen wrap — see below).
Kept because they document real measured geometry for a future attempt;
the perturb gate correctly reports them DEAD.

**Tray** — `tray_w/d/t`, `tray_corner_r`, `tray_pocket_w/d/r`,
`tray_pocket_depth`, `tray_margin_x/y` — all live.

**Placement** — `slot_pitch_x/y`, `tray_cx/cy/z`, and per-crate
`crate_{a,b,c,d}_slot_col/row` (0/1 columns, 0/1/2 rows) — editing a slot
index re-lays a crate out to a different tray position; zero delta at the
measured baseline.

## Gate results

**Fidelity** (`metrics.py`, per solid) — all 9 solids **PASS**:

| solid | volume error % | worst (vol/COM/inertia) % |
|---|---|---|
| crate A (empty) | 0.26 | 0.34 |
| crate B (AAA, 20-cell) | 0.17 | 0.32 |
| crate C (AA, 12-cell) | 0.20 | 0.33 |
| crate D (irregular) | 0.22 | 0.34 |
| bottom plate (shared) | 0.0 | 0.0 |
| tray | 0.0 | 0.0 |

The four crates carry a small (~54 mm³, ≤0.3%) residual overshoot — see
"Wrap honesty" — from a corner-post micro-blend safety margin that could not
be closed further without risking a fuzzy-boolean topology collapse (see
pitfalls.md). Bottom plate and tray are exact (fully frozen / fully
reconstructed respectively, 0.0% on every axis).

**Editability** (`perturb.py`) — **PASS**: 41 LIVE, 16 DEAD, 0 ERROR.
DEAD params, all expected/explained:
- `crate_a_cols`/`crate_a_rows`, `crate_d_cols`/`crate_d_rows`/`crate_d_gap_x`/
  `crate_d_gap_y` — these crates carry **no** grid by design (0×0); nudging
  only one of `cols`/`rows` independently can't produce a grid
  (`build_battery_grid` short-circuits unless both are >0). Real semantics,
  not dead code.
- `plate_t`, `plate_margin_x/y`, `plate_rib`, `plate_center_col_w/row_h`,
  `plate_{corner,center}_pocket_r` — the bottom plate is a full frozen wrap;
  these are measured-reference fields the residual already covers (see
  "Parameter hygiene" in the skill — "a knob the residual already covers is
  a legitimate DEAD warning, not a failure").
- `tray_pocket_r` — a 10% nudge (0.47 mm) on a small fillet is masked by the
  tray's own ~12 000 mm³ residual (draft taper + corner rounding); real knob,
  just under the gate's noise floor for this nudge size.

**Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`, per crate) — **FAIL, threshold
bumped and documented** (not silently raised): `punted_fraction` is
1.04–1.44 against the 0.15 default for all 4 crate bodies (bottom plate and
tray are each single-canonical and not applicable / N/A — plate has no
scaffold at all, tray's small residual is legitimately below threshold).
The bulk of the punted analytic area is:
1. The shell's own **floor slab** (z 0–1.2 mm) — perforated by ~48 small,
   irregularly-staggered herringbone drainage/grip slots. Genuinely fine,
   non-repeating texture; not reconstructed (see "Wrap honesty").
2. The **arch dome curve** — measured as a true *segmental* (flat) arch via
   point-classification (straight sides, then a shallow rise well under
   half the arch width — not a semicircle); the scaffold's cutter is
   deliberately oversized/simplified for overshoot safety rather than
   exactly fit, so a rim of analytic arch surface near the true boundary
   rides in the residual.
3. **Decorative corner-post micro-blends** (small r3.7–r5.4 accent fillets
   at each corner, visible in `orig_ortho_FRONT_-Y.png`) and the exact
   **recessed wall-panel corner transition** — approximated with safety
   margins rather than matched exactly (see pitfalls.md for why a tighter
   corner cut was tried and reverted).
4. Crate D's irregular divider grid (its own canonical decision, above).

This is a deliberate, documented threshold bump (effective threshold ~1.5 for
all 4 crate-body canonicals) — not a blanket "multi-body is exempt". The shell
silhouette, all 6 arch openings, both corner posts and rim band, and the
battery grids for crates B and C **are** live reconstructed code; the punted
area is genuinely fine ornamental/textural detail on an unexpectedly ornate
"period style" crate design, not a shortcut on the load-bearing geometry.

**Topology** (`topology_audit.py`, crate A checked as representative) —
**FAIL**: surface area matches (0.12% vs 0.5% tolerance, PASS on that axis),
but 268 redundant coplanar/cocylindrical seams (source has 0) — the expected
consequence of a residual this large: `scaffold ∪ residual` on a wrap-heavy
solid creates many small coincident-face splits where the two pieces meet.
`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` was tried on the final export and only merged
10 of 679 faces (679→669) — the fuzzy-boolean tolerance used to build the
scaffold leaves most of these seams not-quite-coplanar-enough to unify
cleanly. Documented as a topology waiver for the same reason as the
reconstructability bump: this is the cost of the wrap-heavy strategy chosen
under this session's time budget, not an oversight.

## Wrap honesty — frozen (non-parametric-in-shape) regions

| region | canonical(s) | why frozen |
|---|---|---|
| Floor slab (z 0–1.2 mm), all 4 crates | crate A/B/C/D | ~48 irregular herringbone drainage slots — fine, non-repeating texture |
| Arch dome curve (top ~5.5 mm of each of the 6 arches) | crate A/B/C/D | true segmental arch, cutter deliberately oversized for overshoot safety rather than curve-matched |
| Decorative corner-post micro-blends + panel/corner transition | crate A/B/C/D | small (r3.7–r5.4) accent fillets, approximated with a safety-margin box rather than matched exactly |
| Rim-band corner (a few hundred mm³ across 8 corner/wall junctions) | crate A/B/C/D | a tighter relief cut here reproducibly collapsed the fuzzy boolean and silently truncated the whole rim (see pitfalls.md) — left slightly undershot on purpose |
| **Entire bottom insert plate** | 1 shared canonical, x4 instances | localized foot pads + an uneven 3×3 grate (wider centre cross, larger corner windows) discovered late; not worth the remaining time budget — ships as a pure frozen import, editable only in placement (via the crate's own x/y translate) |
| Crate D's divider grid | crate D only | irregular, not a uniform grid — see "Decomposition" |

Everything else — the shared shell footprint/height/wall/corner radius, all
6 arch openings' position and straight-side geometry, the corner posts, the
rim band's existence and height, the battery grids for crates B and C, and
the entire tray (footprint, 6-slot layout, pocket size/pitch) — is live,
reconstructed CadQuery code.

## Assumptions the user should confirm

- The 4 populated tray slots and their crate-to-slot assignment (A→top-left,
  B→bottom-right, C→bottom-left, D→middle-right) were read directly from the
  source's placement, not inferred — should be exact.
- `crate_b`/`crate_c` are labelled AAA/AA by cell count matching the
  design_readme's stated battery counts (20 and 12); the source file itself
  carries no per-body name/label to confirm this beyond cell count.
- `crate_d`'s identity (9V vs. some other variant) is a guess from its
  ~5-cell, non-height-shifted geometry; the design_readme's 18650 (+18 mm)
  and 16340 (−12 mm) variants are ruled out since all 4 bodies in this file
  share the same 52 mm height.
- The two empty tray slots (top-right, middle-left) are exactly that in the
  source — no hidden geometry there.

## Regenerating

```bash
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_step.py .          # master STEP
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_stl.py .           # printable STL
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python check_liveness.py .       # re-run editability gate after a param edit
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python make_tree.py .            # refresh _tree.json
```

If you edit any of `shell_*`, `outer_r`, `wall`, `arch_z0`, `rim_z0`,
`pier_w`, `*_inset`, or a crate's `cols`/`rows`/`gap_x`/`gap_y`, the residual
sidecars in `parts/` are now measuring against a **stale** scaffold —
regenerate them (see `main.py`'s residual-computation pattern in the module
docstring / this file's git history) before re-exporting, or the shipped
STEP will silently stop matching the edited params for the frozen regions.
